# Model testin
Here are 7 single-turn messages from the use-case doc, with what a good run should look like. The first 6 test search and discovery. The 7th is the only one that should return a query, so it's the check for the validation and promotion work.

# Environment settings:
`AI_MCP_MODE=gateway`
`AI_MODEL_PROVIDER=aws-sdk`
`BEDROCK_REGION=us-east-1`
`BEDROCK_MODEL_ID=openai.gpt-oss-20b-1:0`
`BEDROCK_MAX_TOKENS=4096`

# Test Case 1:
Use case: Synonyms (UC2)
Message: "Find variables related to BMI"
What a good run looks like: Searches, retries with "body mass index" if needed, and lists real variable names.
Returns: No query, prose only.

  "response": "The PIC‑SURE data dictionary is a catalog of all variables (called *concepts*) that are available in the data platform.  \nFor each concept it stores open‑access metadata such as:\n\n* **Display name** – a human‑readable label.  \n* **Description** – a short explanation of the variable.  \n* **Data type** – categorical, continuous, or other.  \n* **Value ranges** – for continuous concepts the minimum and maximum observed values; for categorical concepts a list of up to 20 possible values (with a note if more exist).  \n* **Metadata entries** – up to 10 additional pieces of information (each trimmed to 300 characters).  \n\nThe data dictionary can be explored in several ways:\n\n1. **Search** – use `search_concepts` with a free‑text query to find concept paths that match your keywords.  \n2. **Facets** – use `list_facets` to see how concepts are grouped by categories such as study, data type, or other facets.  \n3. **Detail view** – use `get_concept` with a specific `conceptPath` (obtained from a search) to view the full metadata for that variable.\n\nAll of these tools return open‑access metadata only; they do not reveal any participant data."
}

{
  "response": "The PIC‑SURE data dictionary is a catalog of all variables (concepts) that are available in the system. For each concept it records:\n\n* **Concept path** – the unique identifier that is used in queries and in the `get_concept` tool.  \n* **Dataset** – the study or data collection to which the concept belongs.  \n* **Display name** and **description** – a human‑readable label and a short explanation of what the concept measures.  \n* **Type** – whether the concept is categorical, continuous, or a genomic attribute.  \n* For categorical concepts: a list of up to 20 possible values and an indication if more values exist.  \n* For continuous concepts: the observed minimum and maximum values.  \n* Up to 10 metadata entries for each concept, each truncated to 300 characters if longer.\n\nThe dictionary is searchable with free‑text queries using the `search_concepts` tool. Results are paginated (10 per page by default, up to 25) and include the concept path and dataset so you can retrieve full metadata with `get_concept`. It also supports facet listing (`list_facets`) to see how concepts are grouped by study, data type, or other categories."
}

# Test Case 5:
Use case: Facets (UC5, UC6)
Message: "Show me all categorical questionnaire variables"
What a good run looks like: Uses list_facets and ideally proposes facets. The response should have facets only for pairs that list_facets actually returned.
Returns: No query, prose only.

Returns: No query, prose only.

----

# Test Case 7:
Use case: Query proposal
Message: "Show me female participants over 40 and include their BMI"
What a good run looks like: Searches for sex, age and BMI, then calls propose_query with a filter on sex and a range on age. The response has a query with real paths.
Returns: query and prose.

Follow-up run: Send it again, this time with the first response's query in the request body, and the message "narrow that to people over 50". The response should contain only the changed query. The model should keep the sex filter and change min. If it drops the sex filter, that is the problem to fix. not clauses are the same kind of problem (see the open item in PLAN.md).
Returns: query and prose.

# Analysis (2026-10-12)

Model: `openai.gpt-oss-20b-1:0`, gateway mode. Reviewed against each test's "good run" description.

## Results

| Test | Result |
|---|---|
| 1 BMI | Pass (both runs). Retried with "body mass index" and listed the real variable. Run 1 made an unneeded `get_concept` call. |
| 2 Typo + obesity | Run 1 fails, Run 2 passes. Run 1: `propose_query` called twice with an empty `phenotypicClause: {}`, rejected with "Each phenotypic clause must be a filter ... or a subquery", hit the 8-iteration cap (`hitCap=true`) and returned the generic "wasn't able to finish" message. Run 2 returned BMI, waist and trunk fat but never searched height or weight. |
| 3 Cholesterol / demographics | Looks fine in both runs (not checked in detail for demographics grouping). |
| 4 Data dictionary | Fails (both runs). No tool calls (`toolsCalled=[]`); answered from the system prompt. `list_facets` never called. |
| 5 Categorical questionnaire | Fails (both runs). Only `search_concepts` used, `list_facets` never called, no facets proposed. Many rows are questionnaire categories rather than variables (not verified against data). |
| 6 Labs / missing data | No usable result. Both runs returned `model_unavailable`. Run 1: Bedrock 400 `ValidationException` because the model emitted a malformed tool name (`search_con?commentary??<\|end\|><\|start\|>assistant`) that was replayed in history (`toolUse.name` must match `[a-zA-Z0-9_-]+`). Run 2: `SdkClientException: Read timed out`. |
| 7 Query proposal | Partly broken. The query is built correctly (`SEX=female`, `AGE min 40`, BMI in `select`), but `propose_query` only returns `{"received":true}` and the HTTP response is prose only, so no query is returned. Run 1b (follow-up "narrow that to over 50") is blocked. Run 2 also called `count_participants`, which returned "< 10" and was reported as the answer. |

## Work to fix (in order)

### 1. Test 7: return the proposed query in the response
**Correction:** the service code already attached the query (`ToolUseLoopService.respond()`). The earlier runs used a stale image. **Retested on a fresh image (test.md lines 1410-1708): `query` is now returned in every run.**

Retest findings:
- Sex filter kept, `min` changed to 50, in both Run 1b follow-ups. The invented `max: 85` and the `count_participants` call did not recur, so they are not reproducible (non-deterministic, no action for now).
- **Open: JSON dumped into the prose.** The tool description says "never write query JSON in a reply", yet Run 1 and both 1b runs put query JSON (or `query: {...}`) in `response`. One 1b run's whole `response` is raw JSON including `facets` and `search`. Fix: prompt wording, and/or strip JSON-looking text from `response` server-side.
- **Open: follow-up dropped `select` (Run 2's 1b).** The model sent a `query` with only `phenotypicClause`, so the returned query has no BMI in `select`. Decide whether a partial query is acceptable (the client merges it) or the service should merge it with the request's current query before returning.
- **Open: `facets` nested inside `query` (Run 2).** `propose_query` rejected it ("Field 'facets' is not part of this tool's input"), and the model retried successfully on the next call. Minor; consider a clearer error that says facets is a top-level field.
- Run 1 had `"facets": []` and `"search": ""` sent unprompted. Harmless, but check `respond()` doesn't report an empty facet list as a change.

### 2. Test 6: tool-name robustness and timeouts
- Validate or sanitize tool names from the model before they enter conversation history. Drop or retry a malformed tool call instead of failing the whole turn.
- Add a retry for a transient model failure, and review the read timeout for the Bedrock client.
- Re-run Test 6 to get actual results: creatinine, albumin, leukocytes (maps to white blood cell count), D-dimer and sickle cell (should be reported as not available, nothing invented).

### 3. Test 2: empty `phenotypicClause` rejection
- Accept a select-only query (omit or empty clause) in `propose_query`, or give a clearer error that says what to change.
- Prevent the repeat-the-same-call loop that burns the iteration cap.
- Prompt the model to search height, weight and waist for "other obesity measures".

### 4. Tests 4 and 5: `list_facets` is never called
- Strengthen the `list_facets` tool description and/or system prompt: structure questions ("what is the data dictionary") and facet or category questions should call it.
- Constrain facets in the response to pairs `list_facets` actually returned.
- Re-run Tests 4 and 5.
